My appetite hasn't really changed much though, so that isn't a major issue for me.\n\nDoctor: When this gray cloud first started three years ago, did it hit you suddenly and severely all at once, or did it develop as a milder, gradual low mood that slowly became your normal?\n\nPatient: It was definitely a gradual, slow-creeping process rather than a sudden crash. When my work routes changed three years ago, it slowly wore me down until this low, drained feeling just became my \"new normal\" that I blamed on getting older and working a tough job."

However, I don't have any thoughts of death or suicide; it's just a lot of self-blame and feeling like a failure.\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep or your appetite been since these feelings started three years ago?\n\nPatient: My sleep is pretty awful—I lie awake for over an hour with my mind spinning about work, and then I wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning unable to drift back to sleep, leaving me completely exhausted. My appetite hasn't really changed much though, so that isn't a major issue for me.\n\nDoctor: When this gray cloud first started three years ago, did it hit you suddenly and severely all at once, or did it develop as a milder, gradual low mood that slowly became your normal?\n\nPatient: It was definitely a gradual, slow-creeping process rather than a sudden crash. When my work routes changed three years ago, it slowly wore me down until this low, drained feeling just became my \"new normal\" that I blamed on getting older and working a tough job.\n\nDoctor: During these past three years, has the intensity of your low mood and fatigue remained at a steady, constant level, or did it ever become so severe that you were completely unable to work or function?\n\nPatient: It has been a steady, constant drag rather than hitting me in sudden, disabling waves.
